m\geq d/s^{2}
.
We use
\mu^{m}=(1-s^{2})^{m}\leq e^{-s^{2}m}
and apply (
A.21
).
Therefore
\mu^{m}e^{s^{2}m/8}\leq 1
, and we find that

\displaystyle\sum_{m\geq d/s^{2}}\mu^{m}\mathbb{P}[X_{m}\in B_{\sigma}(y)]

\displaystyle\leq\mathsf{C}_{\textup{ac}}e^{-as/2}\sum_{m\geq d/s^{2}}\frac{1}{m^{d/2}}

(A.46)

\displaystyle\lesssim e^{-as/2}s^{d-2}\lesssim a^{-(d-2)}e^{-as/4},

where the last inequality is as in (
A.2
).

This completes the proof.
∎

The following is an example of a regular random walk which does not
obey a pointwise version of (
A.39
).
The averaging in Theorem
A.5
therefore plays an important role.

Example A.6
.

Let
d>2
and
N>1
be integers.
Consider the random walk on
\mathbb{Z}^{d}
whose transition probabilities are given, for
1\leq i\leq d
, by

\mathbb{P}[X_{1}=\pm N\mathbf{e}_{i}]=\frac{1}{2d}
.
Then
\sigma=N
and

M(s)=\mathbb{E}[e^{s(\mathbf{e}_{1}\cdot X_{1})/\sigma}]=\frac{d-1}{d}+\frac{\cosh(s)}{d}.

(A.47)

Therefore
M(1)\leq 1+e
for every
N
, so the
walk is uniformly regular in
N
.
At criticality,
a non-averaged estimate of (
A.39
) would have
to state that

\mathbb{G}_{1}(0,N\mathbf{e}_{1})\leq\frac{\mathsf{C}_{\textup{RW}}}{\sigma^{d}}\left(\frac{\sigma}{\sigma\vee|N\mathbf{e}_{1}|}\right)^{d-2}=\frac{\mathsf{C}_{\textup{RW}}}{N^{d}}.

(A.48)

This cannot hold for every
N
because

\mathbb{G}_{1}(0,N\mathbf{e}_{1})\geq\frac{1}{2d}
.

A.3
Pointwise estimates: proof of Proposition
2.1

We now apply Theorem
A.5
to prove its pointwise counterpart
for any random walk on
\mathbb{Z}^{d}
whose transition function is given by
an admissible kernel, as defined in Definition
1.1
.

Let
J
be an admissible kernel.
In particular, there is a constant
c_{0}>0
such that

c_{0}R_{J}\leq\sigma_{J},\qquad J_{x}\leq c_{0}^{-1}R_{J}^{-d}\quad(x\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}).

(A.49)

Let
\mathbb{P}_{J}
denote the law of the random walk
(X_{k})_{k\geq 0}
started at
0
and of step distribution given by
J
, and let
\sigma_{J}^{2}:=\mathbb{E}_{J}[|X_{1}|_{2}^{2}]
. For
\mu\in[0,1]
and
x\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
, the Green
function is
\mathbb{C}_{\mu}(x)=\sum_{m\geq 0}\mu^{m}\mathbb{P}_{J}[X_{m}=x]
, and we
define the moment generating function
M_{J}(s)=\mathbb{E}_{J}[\exp(s(\mathbf{e}_{1})\cdot X_{1})]
.

We restate Proposition
2.1
here as Proposition
A.7
.
For the nearest-neighbour
J
, it provides a different proof of the Green function
estimate of
[
84
, Proposition 2.1]
.
For the specific spread-out
J
of (
1.12
),
Proposition
A.7
improves the Green function estimate of

[
65
, Proposition B.1]
, which
has less precise control in terms of
\sigma_{J}
.

Proposition A.7
.

Let
d>2
.
There exist
\mathsf{c},\mathsf{C}>0
, which depend on
d
and
c_{0}
but not on
J
, such that for every
\mu\leq 1
, every
m\geq 1
, and every
x\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{J}[X_{m}=x]\leq\frac{\mathsf{C}}{\sigma_{J}^{d}}\frac{1}{m^{d/2}}\exp\left(-\mathsf{c}\frac{|x|}{\sigma_{J}\sqrt{m}}\right),

(A.50)

\displaystyle\mathbb{C}_{\mu}(x)\leq\delta_{0}(x)+\frac{\mathsf{C}}{\sigma_{J}^{d}}\left(\frac{\sigma_{J}}{\sigma_{J}\vee|x|}\right)^{d-2}\exp\left(-\mathsf{c}\sqrt{1-\mu}\frac{|x|}{\sigma_{J}}\right).

(A.51)

Proof.

To begin, we notice that

M_{J}(2/\sigma_{J})=\sum_{x\in\Lambda_{R}}\exp(2(\mathbf{e}_{1}\cdot x)/\sigma_{J})J_{x}\leq\exp(2R_{J}/\sigma_{J})\leq\exp(2/c_{0}),

(A.52)

so the random walk is
(2,\exp(2/c_{0}))
-regular, uniformly in
R_{J}\geq 1
.
We can therefore conclude from Theorem
A.5
that,
with the
d
- and
c_{0}
-dependent
constants
(\mathsf{c}_{\textup{RW}},\mathsf{C}_{\textup{RW}})
determined by
(\mathsf{c}_{\textup{reg}},\mathsf{C}_{\textup{reg}})=(2,\exp(2/c_{0}))
,
and for every
y\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
,

\mathbb{C}_{\mu}(\Lambda_{\sigma_{J}}(y))\leq\mathsf{C}_{\textup{RW}}\left(\frac{\sigma_{J}}{\sigma_{J}\vee|y|}\right)^{d-2}\exp\left(-\mathsf{c}_{\textup{RW}}\sqrt{1-\mu}\frac{|y|}{\sigma_{J}}\right).

(A.53)

We first prove (
A.51
), by
“unaveraging” (
A.53
). For this,
we start by using
J_{x}\leq c_{0}^{-1}R_{J}^{-d}
(by (
A.49
)) and the definition of
R_{J}

to see that, for
m\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{J}[X_{m}=x]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{u\in\Lambda_{R_{J}}(x)\setminus\{x\}}\mathbb{P}_{J}[X_{m-1}=u]J_{u-x}\leq\frac{1}{c_{0}R_{J}^{d}}\mathbb{P}_{J}[X_{m-1}\in\Lambda_{R_{J}}(x)].

(A.54)

The Green function therefore obeys

\displaystyle\mathbb{C}_{\mu}(x)

\displaystyle=\delta_{0}(x)+\sum_{m\geq 1}\mu^{m}\mathbb{P}_{J}[X_{m}=x]\leq\delta_{0}(x)+\frac{1}{c_{0}R_{J}^{d}}{\mathbb{C}}_{\mu}(\Lambda_{R_{J}}(x)).

(A.55)

With (
A.53
), this is almost what we need, but there
is a mismatch between the boxes of size
R_{J}
and
\sigma_{J}
.
To deal with this, we let
b=\lceil c_{0}^{-1}\rceil

and
B=(2b+1)^{d}
, so that, by (
A.53
)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{C}}_{\mu}(\Lambda_{R_{J}}(x))

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j\in\Lambda_{b}}{\mathbb{C}}_{\mu}(\Lambda_{\sigma_{J}}(x+j\sigma))

(A.56)

\displaystyle\leq B\mathsf{C}_{\textup{RW}}\sup_{j\in\Lambda_{b}}\left(\frac{\sigma_{J}}{\sigma_{J}\vee|x+j\sigma_{J}|}\right)^{d-2}\exp\left(-\mathsf{c}_{\textup{RW}}\sqrt{1-\mu}\frac{|x+j\sigma_{J}|}{\sigma_{J}}\right).

Suppose first that
|x|\leq 2b\sigma_{J}
. Then,

\frac{\sigma_{J}}{\sigma_{J}\vee|x+j\sigma_{J}|}\leq 1\leq 2b\frac{\sigma_{J}}{\sigma_{J}\vee|x|},

(A.57)

\exp\left(-\mathsf{c}_{\textup{RW}}\sqrt{1-\mu}\frac{|x+j\sigma_{J}|}{\sigma_{J}}\right)\leq 1\leq e^{\mathsf{c}_{\textup{RW}}2b}\exp\left(-\mathsf{c}_{\textup{RW}}\sqrt{1-\mu}\frac{|x|}{\sigma_{J}}\right).

(A.58)

These give the desired bound for the case
|x|\leq 2b\sigma_{J}
, with suitable constants

\mathsf{c},\mathsf{C}
.
Suppose instead that
|x|\geq 2b\sigma_{J}
. In this case, for every
j\in\Lambda_{b}
,

|x+j\sigma_{J}|\geq|x|-b\sigma_{J}\geq\frac{1}{2}|x|,

(A.59)

which implies the desired bound (
A.51
) with suitable constants

\mathsf{c},\mathsf{C}
.

We prove (
A.50
) similarly.
For
m=1
it is straightforward, so we assume
m\geq 2
.
We start with (
A.54
) and apply the averaged anti-concentration
bound of (
A.19
), and
\sigma_{J}\leq R_{J}
, to obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{J}[X_{m}=x]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{B}{c_{0}\sigma_{J}^{d}}\frac{\mathsf{C}_{\textup{ac}}e^{t_{0}^{2}/8}}{(m-1)^{d/2}}\sup_{j\in\Lambda_{b}}\exp\left(-t_{0}\frac{|x+j\sigma_{J}|}{2\sigma_{J}\sqrt{m-1}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{B}{c_{0}\sigma_{J}^{d}}\frac{2^{d/2}\mathsf{C}_{\textup{ac}}e^{t_{0}^{2}/8}}{m^{d/2}}e^{2t_{0}b}\sup_{j\in\Lambda_{b}}\exp\left(-\frac{t_{0}}{4}\frac{|x|}{\sigma_{J}\sqrt{m}}\right).

(A.60)

This gives (
A.50
) and, after relaxing the values of
\mathsf{c},\mathsf{C}

if necessary, completes the proof.
∎

Appendix B
Convolution estimates

In this appendix, we collect two convolution estimates.
First, we prove Lemma
5.2
, which we restate as
the following lemma.

Lemma B.1
.

Let
a,b,c_{1},c_{2},\sigma,\xi>0
,
\mu>0
and
\varepsilon\in[0,1]
.
Suppose that the functions
f,g:\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to[0,\infty)
satisfy

\displaystyle f(x)

\displaystyle\leq c_{1}\frac{1}{\sigma^{d}}\left(\frac{\sigma}{\sigma\vee|x|}\right)^{d-2-\varepsilon}e^{-a|x|/\xi},

(B.1)

\displaystyle g(\Lambda_{\xi}(x))

\displaystyle\leq c_{2}\left(\frac{\xi}{\xi\vee|x|}\right)^{d-2}e^{-b|x|/\xi}.

(B.2)

Then, there is a constant
C_{a,\mu}
such that, for every
|x|\geq 2(\sigma\vee\xi)
,

\sum_{y\notin\Lambda_{\mu\xi}(0)}f(y)g(x-y)\leq\frac{c_{1}}{\sigma^{2}|x|^{d-2}}\Big(\frac{|x|}{\sigma}\Big)^{\varepsilon}\left(2^{d}\|g\|_{1}e^{-a|x|/2\xi}+c_{2}C_{a,\mu}\Big(\frac{\xi}{|x|}\Big)^{\varepsilon}e^{-b|x|/2\xi}\right).

(B.3)

Proof.

We first observe that we may assume that
\mu\in(0,1)
.
Indeed, if
\mu\geq 1
then we can bound the left-hand side of (
B.3
)
by the left-hand side with
\mu=\frac{1}{2}
. That new left-hand side is bounded by the corresponding right-hand side, which suffices.

The first term in the upper bound on the convolution arises from

\displaystyle\sum_{y\notin\Lambda_{|x|/2}(0)}f(y)g(x-y)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{c_{1}}{\sigma^{d}}\left(\frac{\sigma}{|x|/2}\right)^{d-2-\varepsilon}e^{-a|x|/2\xi}\sum_{y\notin\Lambda_{|x|/2}(0)}g(x-y)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{c_{1}}{\sigma^{2}|x|^{d-2}}\Big(\frac{|x|}{\sigma}\Big)^{\varepsilon}2^{d}e^{-a|x|/2\xi}\|g\|_{1}.

(B.4)

The remaining contribution to (
B.3
) is due to the sum over
y\in\Lambda_{|x|/2}(0)\setminus\Lambda_{\mu\xi}(0)
.
We do not have a
pointwise hypothesis on
g
, so averaging is required.
For
k\geq 0
, we define the annulus
A_{k}:=\{z\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}:2^{k}\mu\xi<|z|\leq 2^{k+1}\mu\xi\}
. We decompose the annulus

\Lambda_{|x|/2}(0)\setminus\Lambda_{\mu\xi}(0)

into the annuli
A_{k}
, with
0\leq k\leq\log_{2}(|x|/2\mu\xi)-1
.
This gives

\sum_{y\in\Lambda_{|x|/2}(0)}f(y)g(x-y)\leq\sum_{k=0}^{\log_{2}(|x|/2\mu\xi)-1}\sum_{y\in A_{k}}f(y)g(x-y).

(B.5)

By hypothesis, if
k\geq 0
and
y\in A_{k}
, we have the uniform bound

f(y)\leq\frac{c_{1}}{\sigma^{d}}\left(\frac{\sigma}{\sigma\vee 2^{k}\xi\mu}\right)^{d-2-\varepsilon}e^{-a2^{k}\mu\xi/\xi}\leq\frac{c_{1}}{\sigma^{2+\varepsilon}}\left(\frac{1}{2^{k}\xi\mu}\right)^{d-2-\varepsilon}e^{-a2^{k}\mu}.

(B.6)

Also, since
\Lambda_{\mu\xi}(x-y)\subset\Lambda_{\xi}(x-y)
,

\displaystyle\sum_{y\in A_{k}}g(x-y)

\displaystyle\leq 2^{kd}\sup_{y\in A_{k}}g(\Lambda_{\mu\xi}(x-y))\leq 2^{kd}\sup_{y\in A_{k}}g(\Lambda_{\xi}(x-y)).

(B.7)

It follows from
the hypothesis on
g
, and the fact that
|y|\leq|x|/2
,
that

\sup_{y\in A_{k}}g(\Lambda_{\xi}(x-y))\leq c_{2}\left(\frac{\xi}{|x|/2}\right)^{d-2}e^{-b|x|/2\xi}.

(B.8)

We abbreviate the notation for the annulus of interest
by defining
A:=\{y\in\Lambda_{|x|/2}(0)\setminus\Lambda_{\mu\xi}(0)\}
.
Altogether, we find that

\displaystyle\sum_{y\in A}f(y)g(x-y)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{c_{1}}{\sigma^{2+\varepsilon}}\left(\frac{1}{|x|}\right)^{d-2-\varepsilon}\left(\frac{\xi}{|x|}\right)^{\varepsilon}c_{2}e^{-b|x|/2\xi}

\displaystyle\quad\times\Big[\left(\frac{2}{\mu}\right)^{d-2}\sum_{k\geq 0}e^{-a2^{k}\mu}2^{k(2+\varepsilon)}\Big].

(B.9)

The factor in square brackets is bounded by a constant
C_{a,\mu}
which depends on
a,\mu,d
.
We therefore obtain the desired upper bound

\frac{c_{1}}{\sigma^{2}|x|^{d-2}}\left(\frac{|x|}{\sigma}\right)^{\varepsilon}c_{2}C_{a,\mu}\left(\frac{\xi}{|x|}\right)^{\varepsilon}e^{-b|x|/2\xi}.

(B.10)

This completes the proof.
∎

Next, we prove Lemma
5.4
, which we restate as follows.

Lemma B.2
.

Let
p,a>0
.
For
i=1,2
, suppose that
f_{i}\in\ell^{1}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})

satisfy
0\leq f_{i}(x)\leq a(1\vee|x|)^{-p}
for all
x\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
.
Let
k\geq 1
. Then

(f_{1}*f_{2})(x)\leq\frac{a}{(1\vee|x|)^{p}}\Big(\frac{1}{k^{p}}\|f_{1}\|_{1}+2^{p}\sum_{y\in\Lambda_{k|x|}(0)}(f_{1}(y)+f_{2}(y))\Big).

(B.11)

Proof.

We divide the sum
\sum_{y}f_{1}(x-y)f_{2}(y)
into three parts:

y\in\Lambda_{|x|/2}(x),\qquad y\in\Lambda_{k|x|}(0)\setminus\Lambda_{|x|/2}(x),\qquad y\notin\Lambda_{k|x|}(0).

(B.12)

For the first case, we have
|y|\geq|x|/2
, so by hypothesis,
and by
\Lambda_{|x|/2}(0)\subset\Lambda_{k|x|}(0)
,

\displaystyle\sum_{y\in\Lambda_{|x|/2}(x)}f_{1}(x-y)f_{2}(y)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2^{p}a}{(1\vee|x|)^{p}}\sum_{y\in\Lambda_{k|x|}(0)}f_{1}(y).

(B.13)

For the second case,
|x-y|\geq|x|/2
, so

\displaystyle\sum_{y\in\Lambda_{k|x|}(0)\setminus\Lambda_{|x|/2}(x)}f_{1}(x-y)f_{2}(y)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2^{p}a}{(1\vee|x|)^{p}}\sum_{y\in\Lambda_{k|x|}(0)}f_{2}(y).

(B.14)

For the third case,
|y|\geq k|x|
, so

\displaystyle\sum_{y\notin\Lambda_{|x|}(x)}f_{1}(x-y)f_{2}(y)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{a}{k^{p}(1\vee|x|)^{p}}\|f_{1}\|_{1}.

(B.15)

This completes the proof.
∎
